## Summary of Codebase Exploration

### 1. Frontend Structure (web/)

**Pages/Routes:**
- `/` — Dashboard (home page) — main entry point
- `/projects` — Projects list view (card & compact modes)
- `/sources` — Global sources view across all projects
- `/releases` — Releases list
- `/semantic-releases` — Semantic releases/analysis view
- `/subscriptions` — Notification subscriptions
- `/channels` — Notification channels (Slack, Discord, Email, etc.)
- `/todo` — Action items/release notification tracking
- `/onboard` — Repository dependency scanning & quick onboarding
- `/login` — OAuth login page

**Dashboard (Home) - `/app/page.tsx`:**
- Shows **stats cards** (projects, releases, activity metrics)
- **Release trend chart** (time-series visualization)
- **Unified feed** (recent releases across projects)
- **Quick onboard** inline widget (scan repo by URL)
- **Discovery section** (GitHub/Docker Hub search/recommendations)
- Listens to SSE events for real-time updates

### 2. "Source" Data Model (Release Ingestion)

**Source Model** (`/internal/models/source.go`):
A **Source** is what users track — a monitored repository/package in an upstream registry (Docker Hub, GitHub, ECR, GitLab, PyPI).

**Key fields:**
- `id`, `project_id`, `provider` (docker, github, ecr, etc.)
- `repository` (e.g., "sentioxyz/changelogue")
- `poll_interval_seconds` — how often to check for new releases
- `enabled` — active/disabled toggle
- `version_filter_include/exclude` — regex patterns to filter versions
- `exclude_prereleases` — boolean flag
- `last_polled_at`, `last_error` — poll status tracking
- `config` — provider-specific JSON config

**How sources are used:**
- Users create **projects** (logical groups)
- Then add **sources** to projects (specific packages/repos to track)
- Sources are polled on an interval to fetch new releases
- Releases are ingested and stored, optionally analyzed by LLM agent
- Both sources and releases can be viewed at project-level or globally

### 3. API Endpoints (Sources/Trackers)

**Sources endpoints** (in `/internal/api/server.go` + `/internal/api/sources.go`):
```
GET    /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/sources       → List sources for project
POST   /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/sources       → Create new source
GET    /api/v1/sources/{id}                       → Get single source
PUT    /api/v1/sources/{id}                       → Update source config
DELETE /api/v1/sources/{id}                       → Delete source
POST   /api/v1/sources/{id}/poll                  → Manually trigger poll
```

**Projects endpoints:**
```
GET    /api/v1/projects                           → List projects
POST   /api/v1/projects                           → Create project
GET    /api/v1/projects/{id}                      → Get project
PUT    /api/v1/projects/{id}                      → Update project
DELETE /api/v1/projects/{id}                      → Delete project
```

**Other key endpoints:**
- Releases: `GET /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/releases` — releases for project
- Semantic Releases: LLM-analyzed release reports with urgency/recommendations
- Subscriptions: notifications on new releases
- Channels: Slack/Discord/Email notification destinations
- Context Sources: supplementary data sources for agent analysis (separate from ingestion sources)
- Agent: trigger manual analysis runs or view agent activity

### 4. Dashboard/Home Page

**`/app/page.tsx`** shows:
1. **Discovery section** — search GitHub/Docker Hub, add repositories quickly
2. **Quick onboard widget** — inline form to scan a repo for dependencies
3. **Stats cards** — total projects, active sources, releases this week, pending agent work
4. **Release trend chart** — releases over time (7/14/30 days)
5. **Unified feed** — recent releases across all projects in chronological order
6. **Empty state** — if no projects exist, prompts user to create one or import dependencies

All dashboard data refreshes via SSE (Server-Sent Events) when new releases are detected.

### 5. Key Models & Data Flow

**Project hierarchy:**
```
Project (container)
  ├─ Sources (monitored repositories, e.g., "ethers.js" on npm)
  ├─ Releases (ingested from sources)
  ├─ Semantic Releases (LLM-analyzed reports on releases)
  └─ Context Sources (supplementary data for agent, e.g., GitHub org repos)
```

**Release flow:**
1. User creates Project → adds Sources (Docker/GitHub repos)
2. Ingestion orchestrator polls sources periodically
3. New releases are fetched and stored
4. If agent is enabled, LLM analyzes release for urgency/changelog/recommendations
5. Notifications sent to subscribed channels
6. Todos created for high-urgency releases

---

- Frontend pages: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/app/`
- API handlers: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/`
- Models: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/`
- Client types/SDK: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/`

## Summary of GitHub OAuth & Token Storage Investigation

### **1. GitHub OAuth Login Flow**

**OAuth Scopes Requested:**
- Only **`read:org`** scope is requested (line 39 in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/auth/oauth.go`)
- This is minimal—just enough to read org membership for allowlisting

**User Data Stored on Login:**
In the `users` table (lines 191-199 in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go`):
- `id` (UUID, primary key)
- `github_id` (BIGINT, unique)
- `github_login` (VARCHAR)
- `name` (VARCHAR, optional)
- `avatar_url` (TEXT, optional)
- `created_at` / `updated_at` timestamps

**OAuth Callback Flow:**
1. User exchanges code → access token (exchanged but **not stored**)
2. Token fetched temporarily to get user info and orgs
3. Token is discarded after login completes
4. Session cookie created instead (server-side in `sessions` table)

### **2. Token Storage - Current State**

**GitHub OAuth tokens:** NOT stored anywhere
- The access token is used once during callback to fetch user/org info, then discarded (line 66-79 in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/auth/oauth.go`)
- No `github_token` or similar field in the `users` table

**API Key storage:** Exists but separate from OAuth
- `api_keys` table (lines 124-131 in migrations):
  - `key_hash` (SHA-256 hash, UNIQUE)
  - `key_prefix` (first 12 chars for display)
  - `last_used_at` (for tracking)
- Used for Bearer token authentication, not GitHub OAuth

**Token usage in codebase:**
- `internal/onboard/scanner.go` (lines 47-55) accepts a `GITHUB_TOKEN` environment variable for repo scanning
- The scanner uses it for GitHub API calls, but this is **environment-level**, not per-user

### **3. User Model & Session Structure**

**User struct** (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/auth/user.go`):
```go
type User struct {
    ID          string `json:"id"`          // UUID
    GitHubID    int64  `json:"github_id"`
    GitHubLogin string `json:"github_login"`
    Name        string `json:"name,omitempty"`
    AvatarURL   string `json:"avatar_url,omitempty"`
}
```

**Session storage:**
- `sessions` table: `(id, user_id, expires_at, created_at)` — server-side, HttpOnly cookie
- HMAC-signed cookie: `"sessionID.hmacHex"` for tampering protection
- 7-day expiration

### **Key Insights**

1. **No stored GitHub tokens** — Current design uses OAuth only for authentication, not authorization to GitHub APIs
2. **Minimal OAuth scope** — Only `read:org` (GitHub limits exposure)
3. **Separation of concerns** — API keys are independent from user auth; GitHub token access is environment-level via `GITHUB_TOKEN`
4. **Allowlisting via orgs** — Can restrict users by GitHub org membership fetched during login
5. **Session pattern is robust** — HMAC-signed, server-side, with cleanup

This architecture intentionally **does not bind GitHub tokens to individual users**—instead relying on environment-level access for backend operations and user-level auth via sessions.

## Summary: Ingestion Architecture & Source Configuration

### 1. **Ingestion Providers (6 Supported)**

The system implements 6 upstream registry providers via the `IIngestionSource` interface:

| Provider | File | API Endpoint | Key Details |
|----------|------|--------------|------------|
| **Docker Hub** | `dockerhub.go` | `/v2/repositories/{Repository}/tags/` | Fetches latest 25 tags ordered by `last_updated` |
| **GitHub** | `github.go` | `/repos/{Repository}/releases` | Fetches 30 releases; supports auth token via `GITHUB_TOKEN` env var; captures changelog + prerelease flag |
| **PyPI** | `pypi.go` | `/pypi/{Package}/json` | Uses PyPI JSON API; handles yanked versions; stores release URL in metadata |
| **npm** | `npm.go` | `/{Package}` | Registry API returns all versions with publish timestamps; filters metadata keys (`created`, `modified`) |
| **ECR Public** | `ecr_public.go` | `POST /describeImageTags` (Gallery API) | Pagination support; requires `registry_alias/repo_name` format; captures `imagePushedAt` timestamps |
| **GitLab** | `gitlab.go` | `/api/v4/projects/{ProjectID}/releases` | Supports GitLab token via `GITLAB_TOKEN` env var; URL-encodes project paths; captures prerelease flag |

### 2. **Source Data Model & Configuration**

**Database Table: `sources`** (defined in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go`, lines 24–38)

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sources (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id),
    provider VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,              -- "dockerhub", "github", etc.
    repository VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,           -- package/repo name (e.g., "golang" for Docker, "user/repo" for GitHub)
    poll_interval_seconds INT DEFAULT 86400,    -- 24 hours default
    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    config JSONB,                               -- flexible per-provider config
    last_polled_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    last_error TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    UNIQUE(provider, repository)                -- no duplicate (provider, repo) pairs
);
```

**Recent migrations added filtering:**
- `version_filter_include` (TEXT) — include pattern
- `version_filter_exclude` (TEXT) — exclude pattern  
- `exclude_prereleases` (BOOLEAN DEFAULT false) — skip prerelease versions

### 3. **How Sources are Added & Configured**

**Via REST API** (`/api/v1/projects/{projectId}/sources`):
- `POST` creates a new source with provider, repository, poll interval
- `PUT` updates poll interval, enable/disable, filters
- Sources permanently linked to a project (foreign key on `project_id`)

**Factory Function** (`BuildSource` in `loader.go`, lines 87–105):
```go
switch sourceType {
case "dockerhub":   return NewDockerHubSource(...)
case "github":      return NewGitHubSource(...)
case "ecr-public":  return NewECRPublicSource(...)
case "gitlab":      return NewGitLabSource(...)
case "pypi":        return NewPyPISource(...)
case "npm":         return NewNpmSource(...)
}
```

### 4. **Polling & Crawling Architecture**

**Orchestrator Flow** (`orchestrator.go`):

1. **Service Startup**: `NewOrchestrator()` creates polling engine with configurable interval (typically every N seconds)
2. **Dynamic Loader**: On each poll cycle, `SourceLoader.LoadEnabledSources()` queries the DB for all enabled sources with their metadata
3. **Schedule Check**: For each source, checks if `now - last_polled_at >= poll_interval_seconds`
4. **Fetch & Persist**: For each due source:
   - Calls `source.FetchNewReleases(ctx)` → HTTP request to upstream API
   - Returns `[]IngestionResult` (version, changelog, metadata, timestamp)
   - Passes results to `Service.ProcessResults()` which calls `store.IngestRelease()` for each

**Transactional Outbox** (`pgstore.go`):
- Each release insertion is wrapped in a transaction with River job enqueueing
- Atomically inserts to `releases` table + enqueues `NotifyJobArgs` to River queue
- Duplicate versions (unique key violation) are skipped with a debug log

**Result Data Model** (`IngestionResult` in `source.go`):
```go
type IngestionResult struct {
    Repository string            // e.g., "golang" (normalization from provider)
    RawVersion string            // e.g., "v1.25.0" (raw tag/version string)
    Changelog  string            // release notes (GitHub, GitLab only)
    Metadata   map[string]string // flexible: release_url, prerelease, etc.
    Timestamp  time.Time         // when the version was released upstream
}
```

### 5. **Database Schema Cascade**

```
projects
  ├─→ sources (one provider per source row)
  │     └─→ releases (all versions discovered by this source)
  │           └─→ semantic_release_sources (join table)
  │                 └─→ semantic_releases (AI-correlated releases)
  └─→ subscriptions (notification routing)
        └─→ notification_channels (Slack, Discord, email, webhooks)
```

**Key Constraints:**
- `releases` has UNIQUE(source_id, version) — prevents duplicate version ingestion
- Release notification is automatic via River's transactional outbox pattern
- Polling sources are never manually written through API; created exclusively via REST source registration

### 6. **Error Handling & Resilience**

- HTTP errors mapped to human-readable messages (`httpStatusError` in `source.go`)
- Rate limiting (HTTP 429) gracefully handled with retry logic in River
- Failed polls update `sources.last_error` field for debugging
- Duplicate releases silently skipped (debug log), not fatal
- Context timeout propagated through all HTTP calls

---

This architecture ensures scalability (horizontal polling), transactional consistency (no event loss), and operator visibility (poll status per source) within a single binary deployment.

## Summary: Existing Discovery, Suggestion, and Onboarding Features

I've thoroughly analyzed the Changelogue codebase. Here's what already exists for discovering and suggesting repositories/dependencies:

---

### **1. Discovery Section (Frontend)**

**Location:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/components/dashboard/discovery-section.tsx`

This is a **marquee carousel** on the dashboard home page that shows trending repositories:
- Displays trending GitHub and Docker Hub repositories in a horizontal scrolling ticker
- Shows repo name, star count, and provider icon
- **One-click tracking**: Users can click a "Star + Plus" button to immediately track a repo
  - Creates a new project
  - Adds a source with daily polling interval (86400 seconds)
  - Auto-polls the source once
  - Refreshes the projects list

**Features:**
- Hover to pause scrolling
- Already-tracked repos show a checkmark and are visually de-emphasized
- Caches trending data for 1 hour to reduce API calls
- Shows loading skeleton while fetching

---

### **2. Discovery API (Backend)**

**Location:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/discover.go`

Two endpoints exposed via `/discover/` routes (no auth required):

#### `GET /api/v1/discover/github`
- **Query params:**
  - `q` — custom search query (optional)
  - `language` — filter by programming language (optional)
- **Default behavior:** Returns trending GitHub repos from the last 7 days with 10+ stars, sorted by star count
- Returns up to 25 results
- Caches results for 1 hour
- Example response fields: `name`, `full_name`, `description`, `stars`, `language`, `url`, `avatar_url`, `provider`

#### `GET /api/v1/discover/dockerhub`
- **Query params:** `q` — search term (defaults to "nginx")
- Returns up to 25 Docker Hub repositories
- Caches results for 1 hour
- Response fields: `name`, `full_name`, `description`, `stars`, `url`, `provider`

---

### **3. Quick Onboard (Dependency Scanner)**

**Frontend:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/app/onboard/page.tsx`

A dedicated onboarding wizard with 4 steps:

**Step 1: Input**
- User enters GitHub repo URL (accepts `owner/repo` or full HTTPS URL)
- Can be triggered from dashboard quick-onboard bar or via query string `?repo=`

**Step 2: Scanning**
- Shows progress indicators (Queued → Fetching files → Extracting deps)
- Elapsed time counter
- Uses River job queue (async background processing)

**Step 3: Results**
- Table showing detected dependencies with:
  - Checkboxes to select which to track (all selected by default)
  - Dependency name, version, ecosystem badge, upstream repo
  - Dropdown to assign each dependency to existing project or create new one
  - Ecosystem-specific color badges (Go, NPM, PyPI, Cargo, etc.)
- "Track Selected" button to apply selections

**Step 4: Applied**
- Shows success confirmation with count of created sources/projects
- Lists any skipped dependencies
- Links to view projects

**Supported Dependency Files:**
- `go.mod`, `package.json`, `requirements.txt`, `Pipfile`, `pyproject.toml`, `Cargo.toml`, `Gemfile`, `pom.xml`, `build.gradle`, `build.gradle.kts`, `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.yaml`

---

### **4. Onboarding Workflow (Backend)**

**Location:**
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/onboard/scanner.go` — Fetches dependency files from GitHub
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/onboard/extractor.go` — LLM-powered dependency extraction (Gemini or OpenAI)
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/onboard/worker.go` — River job worker
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/onboard.go` — HTTP handlers

**Process:**
1. **POST `/api/v1/onboard/scan`** → Creates an `OnboardScan` record with status "pending"
   - Prevents concurrent scans for same repo (returns 409 if active scan exists)
   - Enqueues a `ScanDependenciesJobArgs` River job
2. **Worker picks up job** → Transitions to "processing"
   - Parses GitHub repo URL
   - Fetches all dependency files via GitHub API (recursive tree view)
   - Extracts dependencies using LLM prompt (Gemini 2.0 Flash by default, OpenAI 4o-mini as fallback)
   - Stores results as JSON array of `ScannedDependency` objects
   - Updates scan status to "completed" or "failed"
   - Sends PG NOTIFY on `release_events` channel
3. **GET `/api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}`** → Polls scan status (frontend polls every 2 seconds)
4. **POST `/api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply`** → User applies selections
   - Takes array of `OnboardSelection` objects
   - For each selection: creates Project + Source (or adds to existing project)
   - Returns counts of created resources and any skipped entries

**LLM Extraction:**
- Uses system prompt to enforce JSON array output (no markdown, no explanations)
- Extracts: name, version, ecosystem, upstream_repo (GitHub best-guess), provider (github/dockerhub/gitlab/ecr_public)
- Supports both Gemini and OpenAI APIs
- Handles markdown-fenced and raw JSON responses

---

### **5. Dashboard Integration**

**Location:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/app/page.tsx`

Three discovery/onboarding entry points:

2. **Quick Onboard inline bar** — Text input + "Scan" button

3. **DashboardEmptyState** — When no projects exist, shows "Create a Project" button linking to `/projects`

**Location:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/client.ts`

Exposed client methods:

discover.dockerhub(params?: { q?: string })
onboard.scan(repoUrl: string)
onboard.getScan(id: string)
onboard.apply(id: string, selections: OnboardSelection[])
```

### **Key Types**

```typescript
// Discovery
DiscoverItem: { name, full_name, description, stars, language?, url, avatar_url?, provider }

// Onboarding
OnboardScan: { id, repo_url, status, results?, error?, created_at, started_at?, completed_at? }
ScannedDependency: { name, version, ecosystem, upstream_repo, provider }
OnboardSelection: { dep_name, upstream_repo, provider, project_id?, new_project_name? }
```

     1→# Visual Companion Guide
     2→
     3→Browser-based visual brainstorming companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and options.
     4→
     5→## When to Use
     6→
     7→Decide per-question, not per-session. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**
     8→
     9→**Use the browser** when the content itself is visual:
    10→
    11→- **UI mockups** — wireframes, layouts, navigation structures, component designs
    12→- **Architecture diagrams** — system components, data flow, relationship maps
    13→- **Side-by-side visual comparisons** — comparing two layouts, two color schemes, two design directions
    14→- **Design polish** — when the question is about look and feel, spacing, visual hierarchy
    15→- **Spatial relationships** — state machines, flowcharts, entity relationships rendered as diagrams
    16→
    17→**Use the terminal** when the content is text or tabular:
    18→
    19→- **Requirements and scope questions** — "what does X mean?", "which features are in scope?"
    20→- **Conceptual A/B/C choices** — picking between approaches described in words
    21→- **Tradeoff lists** — pros/cons, comparison tables
    22→- **Technical decisions** — API design, data modeling, architectural approach selection
    23→- **Clarifying questions** — anything where the answer is words, not a visual preference
    24→
    25→A question *about* a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What kind of wizard do you want?" is conceptual — use the terminal. "Which of these wizard layouts feels right?" is visual — use the browser.
    26→
    27→## How It Works
    28→
    29→The server watches a directory for HTML files and serves the newest one to the browser. You write HTML content, the user sees it in their browser and can click to select options. Selections are recorded to a `.events` file that you read on your next turn.
    30→
    31→**Content fragments vs full documents:** If your HTML file starts with `<!DOCTYPE` or `<html`, the server serves it as-is (just injects the helper script). Otherwise, the server automatically wraps your content in the frame template — adding the header, CSS theme, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure. **Write content fragments by default.** Only write full documents when you need complete control over the page.
    32→
    33→## Starting a Session
    34→
    35→```bash
    36→# Start server with persistence (mockups saved to project)
    37→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    38→
    39→# Returns: {"type":"server-started","port":52341,"url":"http://localhost:52341",
    40→#           "screen_dir":"/path/to/project/.superpowers/brainstorm/12345-1706000000"}
    41→```
    42→
    43→Save `screen_dir` from the response. Tell user to open the URL.
    44→
    45→**Finding connection info:** The server writes its startup JSON to `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info`. If you launched the server in the background and didn't capture stdout, read that file to get the URL and port. When using `--project-dir`, check `<project>/.superpowers/brainstorm/` for the session directory.
    46→
    47→**Note:** Pass the project root as `--project-dir` so mockups persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` and survive server restarts. Without it, files go to `/tmp` and get cleaned up. Remind the user to add `.superpowers/` to `.gitignore` if it's not already there.
    48→
    49→**Launching the server by platform:**
    50→
    51→**Claude Code (macOS / Linux):**
    52→```bash
    53→# Default mode works — the script backgrounds the server itself
    54→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    55→```
    56→
    57→**Claude Code (Windows):**
    58→```bash
    59→# Windows auto-detects and uses foreground mode, which blocks the tool call.
    60→# Use run_in_background: true on the Bash tool call so the server survives
    61→# across conversation turns.
    62→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    63→```
    64→When calling this via the Bash tool, set `run_in_background: true`. Then read `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` on the next turn to get the URL and port.
    65→
    66→**Codex:**
    67→```bash
    68→# Codex reaps background processes. The script auto-detects CODEX_CI and
    69→# switches to foreground mode. Run it normally — no extra flags needed.
    70→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    71→```
    72→
    73→**Gemini CLI:**
    74→```bash
    75→# Use --foreground and set is_background: true on your shell tool call
    76→# so the process survives across turns
    77→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project --foreground
    78→```
    79→
    80→**Other environments:** The server must keep running in the background across conversation turns. If your environment reaps detached processes, use `--foreground` and launch the command with your platform's background execution mechanism.
    81→
    82→If the URL is unreachable from your browser (common in remote/containerized setups), bind a non-loopback host:
    83→
    84→```bash
    85→scripts/start-server.sh \
    86→  --project-dir /path/to/project \
    87→  --host 0.0.0.0 \
    88→  --url-host localhost
    89→```
    90→
    91→Use `--url-host` to control what hostname is printed in the returned URL JSON.
    92→
    93→## The Loop
    94→
    95→1. **Check server is alive**, then **write HTML** to a new file in `screen_dir`:
    96→   - Before each write, check that `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` exists. If it doesn't (or `.server-stopped` exists), the server has shut down — restart it with `start-server.sh` before continuing. The server auto-exits after 30 minutes of inactivity.
    97→   - Use semantic filenames: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
    98→   - **Never reuse filenames** — each screen gets a fresh file
    99→   - Use Write tool — **never use cat/heredoc** (dumps noise into terminal)
   100→   - Server automatically serves the newest file
   101→
   102→2. **Tell user what to expect and end your turn:**
   103→   - Remind them of the URL (every step, not just first)
   104→   - Give a brief text summary of what's on screen (e.g., "Showing 3 layout options for the homepage")
   105→   - Ask them to respond in the terminal: "Take a look and let me know what you think. Click to select an option if you'd like."
   106→
   107→3. **On your next turn** — after the user responds in the terminal:
   108→   - Read `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` if it exists — this contains the user's browser interactions (clicks, selections) as JSON lines
   109→   - Merge with the user's terminal text to get the full picture
   110→   - The terminal message is the primary feedback; `.events` provides structured interaction data
   111→
   112→4. **Iterate or advance** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new file (e.g., `layout-v2.html`). Only move to the next question when the current step is validated.
   113→
   114→5. **Unload when returning to terminal** — when the next step doesn't need the browser (e.g., a clarifying question, a tradeoff discussion), push a waiting screen to clear the stale content:
   115→
   116→   ```html
   117→   <!-- filename: waiting.html (or waiting-2.html, etc.) -->
   118→   <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;min-height:60vh">
   119→     <p class="subtitle">Continuing in terminal...</p>
   120→   </div>
   121→   ```
   122→
   123→   This prevents the user from staring at a resolved choice while the conversation has moved on. When the next visual question comes up, push a new content file as usual.
   124→
   125→6. Repeat until done.
   126→
   127→## Writing Content Fragments
   128→
   129→Write just the content that goes inside the page. The server wraps it in the frame template automatically (header, theme CSS, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure).
   130→
   131→**Minimal example:**
   132→
   133→```html
   134→<h2>Which layout works better?</h2>
   135→<p class="subtitle">Consider readability and visual hierarchy</p>
   136→
   137→<div class="options">
   138→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   139→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   140→    <div class="content">
   141→      <h3>Single Column</h3>
   142→      <p>Clean, focused reading experience</p>
   143→    </div>
   144→  </div>
   145→  <div class="option" data-choice="b" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   146→    <div class="letter">B</div>
   147→    <div class="content">
   148→      <h3>Two Column</h3>
   149→      <p>Sidebar navigation with main content</p>
   150→    </div>
   151→  </div>
   152→</div>
   153→```
   154→
   155→That's it. No `<html>`, no CSS, no `<script>` tags needed. The server provides all of that.
   156→
   157→## CSS Classes Available
   158→
   159→The frame template provides these CSS classes for your content:
   160→
   161→### Options (A/B/C choices)
   162→
   163→```html
   164→<div class="options">
   165→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   166→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   167→    <div class="content">
   168→      <h3>Title</h3>
   169→      <p>Description</p>
   170→    </div>
   171→  </div>
   172→</div>
   173→```
   174→
   175→**Multi-select:** Add `data-multiselect` to the container to let users select multiple options. Each click toggles the item. The indicator bar shows the count.
   176→
   177→```html
   178→<div class="options" data-multiselect>
   179→  <!-- same option markup — users can select/deselect multiple -->
   180→</div>
   181→```
   182→
   183→### Cards (visual designs)
   184→
   185→```html
   186→<div class="cards">
   187→  <div class="card" data-choice="design1" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   188→    <div class="card-image"><!-- mockup content --></div>
   189→    <div class="card-body">
   190→      <h3>Name</h3>
   191→      <p>Description</p>
   192→    </div>
   193→  </div>
   194→</div>
   195→```
   196→
   197→### Mockup container
   198→
   199→```html
   200→<div class="mockup">
   201→  <div class="mockup-header">Preview: Dashboard Layout</div>
   202→  <div class="mockup-body"><!-- your mockup HTML --></div>
   203→</div>
   204→```
   205→
   206→### Split view (side-by-side)
   207→
   208→```html
   209→<div class="split">
   210→  <div class="mockup"><!-- left --></div>
   211→  <div class="mockup"><!-- right --></div>
   212→</div>
   213→```
   214→
   215→### Pros/Cons
   216→
   217→```html
   218→<div class="pros-cons">
   219→  <div class="pros"><h4>Pros</h4><ul><li>Benefit</li></ul></div>
   220→  <div class="cons"><h4>Cons</h4><ul><li>Drawback</li></ul></div>
   221→</div>
   222→```
   223→
   224→### Mock elements (wireframe building blocks)
   225→
   226→```html
   227→<div class="mock-nav">Logo | Home | About | Contact</div>
   228→<div style="display: flex;">
   229→  <div class="mock-sidebar">Navigation</div>
   230→  <div class="mock-content">Main content area</div>
   231→</div>
   232→<button class="mock-button">Action Button</button>
   233→<input class="mock-input" placeholder="Input field">
   234→<div class="placeholder">Placeholder area</div>
   235→```
   236→
   237→### Typography and sections
   238→
   239→- `h2` — page title
   240→- `h3` — section heading
   241→- `.subtitle` — secondary text below title
   242→- `.section` — content block with bottom margin
   243→- `.label` — small uppercase label text
   244→
   245→## Browser Events Format
   246→
   247→When the user clicks options in the browser, their interactions are recorded to `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` (one JSON object per line). The file is cleared automatically when you push a new screen.
   248→
   249→```jsonl
   250→{"type":"click","choice":"a","text":"Option A - Simple Layout","timestamp":1706000101}
   251→{"type":"click","choice":"c","text":"Option C - Complex Grid","timestamp":1706000108}
   252→{"type":"click","choice":"b","text":"Option B - Hybrid","timestamp":1706000115}
   253→```
   254→
   255→The full event stream shows the user's exploration path — they may click multiple options before settling. The last `choice` event is typically the final selection, but the pattern of clicks can reveal hesitation or preferences worth asking about.
   256→
   257→If `.events` doesn't exist, the user didn't interact with the browser — use only their terminal text.
   258→
   259→## Design Tips
   260→
   261→- **Scale fidelity to the question** — wireframes for layout, polish for polish questions
   262→- **Explain the question on each page** — "Which layout feels more professional?" not just "Pick one"
   263→- **Iterate before advancing** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new version
   264→- **2-4 options max** per screen
   265→- **Use real content when it matters** — for a photography portfolio, use actual images (Unsplash). Placeholder content obscures design issues.
   266→- **Keep mockups simple** — focus on layout and structure, not pixel-perfect design
   267→
   268→## File Naming
   269→
   270→- Use semantic names: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
   271→- Never reuse filenames — each screen must be a new file
   272→- For iterations: append version suffix like `layout-v2.html`, `layout-v3.html`
   273→- Server serves newest file by modification time
   274→
   275→## Cleaning Up
   276→
   277→```bash
   278→scripts/stop-server.sh $SCREEN_DIR
   279→```
   280→
   281→If the session used `--project-dir`, mockup files persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` for later reference. Only `/tmp` sessions get deleted on stop.
   282→
   283→## Reference
   284→
   285→- Frame template (CSS reference): `scripts/frame-template.html`
   286→- Helper script (client-side): `scripts/helper.js`
   287→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

> AGENT

Good, the visual companion is running. Now let me start with the first clarifying question. Since this is a conceptual/scope question, I'll use the terminal.

Great, visual companion is live at **http://localhost:52861** — open that in your browser. I'll use it when we get to layout/UI decisions.

Now let me make sure I understand what you're envisioning. I explored the codebase and here's what already exists:

- **Discovery carousel** — shows trending GitHub/Docker Hub repos on the dashboard
- **Quick onboard scanner** — paste a GitHub repo URL, it scans dependency files with LLM, lets you bulk-track them
- **GitHub OAuth** — stores `github_id`, `github_login`, but **does not store the user's access token** (it's discarded after login)

Your idea adds two new **personalized** dimensions:
1. **Starred repos** — show the user's GitHub stars as tracking suggestions
2. **Recently active repos** — crawl repos the user works on and suggest their dependencies

Here's my first question:

**For the "starred repos" feature — do you mean the user's own GitHub starred repositories (repos they've clicked the star button on), or something else?** And for the "recently working projects" — do you mean the user's own GitHub repos (ones they own/contribute to), and then scanning those for their dependency files (like `go.mod`, `package.json`, etc.)?

> DEVELOPER

yes, the user's own GitHub starred repositories and repos they own/contribute to, resue the same llm dependencies analyze

> AGENT

Good, that confirms the scope. Now an important technical question.

Currently, the GitHub OAuth token is **discarded after login** — only `read:org` scope is requested, and the token isn't stored. To fetch a user's starred repos, their own repos, and repo contents for dependency scanning, we'd need to:

1. **Request additional OAuth scopes** (at minimum `read:user` for stars, `repo` or `public_repo` for accessing repo contents)
2. **Store the GitHub access token** in the database tied to the user

**How do you feel about the scope of GitHub access?** Should we:

- **A) Public data only** — request just enough to read the user's public stars and public repos (lower friction, less permissions)
- **B) Include private repos** — request `repo` scope to also see private repos and their dependencies (more useful, but users may hesitate to grant broad access)

> DEVELOPER

Let's start with A then in the future we will support B

> AGENT

Good call — start minimal, expand later. Next question:

**How should this feature be triggered and presented?** Right now the dashboard already has the trending discovery carousel and the quick onboard scanner. I'm thinking about where the personalized suggestions fit. Let me show you some layout options.

> AGENT

Here's a summary of decisions so far, then my proposed approaches.

## Decisions So Far

- **OAuth**: Store the GitHub token, request public-only scopes (`read:user`, `public_repo` deferred — just public API access)
- **Placement**: Replace trending carousel with personalized suggestions for authenticated users; fall back to trending for unauthenticated
- **Trigger**: On demand — user clicks to fetch
- **Layout**: Two tabs — "Your Stars" and "Your Dependencies"
- **Dependencies tab flow**: Show user's repos as a checklist → they pick which to scan → reuse existing LLM dependency extraction
- **Stars tab**: Show starred repos with one-click track

## Proposed Approaches

### Approach 1: Minimal — GitHub public API, no token storage (Recommended)

Use the **unauthenticated GitHub API** (or a single server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN`) to fetch user data by username (which we already have as `github_login`). No OAuth scope changes needed.

- `GET /users/{login}/starred` — public stars (public repos only)
- `GET /users/{login}/repos` — public repos

**Pros**: No OAuth changes, no token storage, no security surface. Ships fast.
**Cons**: Can't see private stars or private repos. Rate limited to 60 req/hr unauthenticated (but server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN` gives 5000/hr). Only public data.

### Approach 2: Store OAuth token, expand scopes

Change OAuth flow to request `read:user` + `public_repo` scopes. Store the encrypted token in the `users` table. Use per-user tokens for GitHub API calls.

- Full access to user's starred repos (including private stars)
- Full access to user's repos (public only with `public_repo`)
- Per-user rate limits (5000/hr per user)

**Pros**: Per-user rate limits, can see private stars, future-proof for private repo support (scope B).
**Cons**: Need to store tokens securely, handle token expiry/refresh, more complex auth flow, migration needed.

### Approach 3: Hybrid — public API now, token storage later

Start with Approach 1 for the initial release. Build the feature around `github_login` and public APIs. Later add token storage as an opt-in "Connect GitHub" step for enhanced access.

**Pros**: Ships fastest with zero auth changes. Clean upgrade path. Separation of concerns.
**Cons**: Two implementation passes. Some users might expect private repo access.

---

**My recommendation: Approach 1 (Minimal).** Since you said public data only for now, we don't actually need to store the token at all. The user's `github_login` is already in the DB — we can fetch their public stars and public repos using the server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN` that the onboard scanner already uses. Zero OAuth changes, zero migrations for token storage. When you're ready for private repo support later, we add token storage then.

What do you think?

> DEVELOPER

Approach 1

## Design Section 1: Backend API

Two new endpoints under `/api/v1/suggestions/`:

**`GET /api/v1/suggestions/stars`**
- Requires authenticated session
- Fetches `GET https://api.github.com/users/{github_login}/starred` using server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN`
- Paginates (GitHub returns 30 per page, we fetch up to ~100)
- Returns list of repos with: `name`, `full_name`, `description`, `stars`, `language`, `url`, `avatar_url`
- Filters out repos already tracked as sources
- Caches per-user for 1 hour (same pattern as existing discovery cache)

**`GET /api/v1/suggestions/repos`**
- Requires authenticated session
- Fetches `GET https://api.github.com/users/{github_login}/repos?sort=pushed&per_page=100`
- Returns list of user's public repos with: `name`, `full_name`, `description`, `language`, `url`, `pushed_at`
- Used to populate the repo picker for dependency scanning

**Dependency scanning** — reuses existing `POST /api/v1/onboard/scan` + `GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}` + `POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply`. No new endpoints needed for the actual scanning.

No new database tables or migrations needed.

Does this section look right so far?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 2: Frontend Flow

The personalized suggestions replace the trending carousel for authenticated users. Here's the interaction flow:

**Initial state (not yet fetched):**
- Where the trending carousel currently sits, show a card: "Discover repos from your GitHub" with two tabs: "Your Stars" | "Your Dependencies"
- Each tab has a button to fetch data ("Load your starred repos" / "Load your repos")

**Stars tab — after fetch:**
- Grid/list of starred repos, each with: name, description, stars count, language badge
- Already-tracked repos shown with a checkmark (same pattern as existing discovery carousel)
- One-click "Track" button per repo (creates project + source, same as discovery carousel logic)
- Pagination or "Load more" if >30 results

**Dependencies tab — multi-step flow:**
1. Click "Load your repos" → fetches repo list from `/api/v1/suggestions/repos`
2. Shows checkboxes list of repos sorted by last pushed. User selects which to scan.
3. Click "Scan selected" → calls `POST /api/v1/onboard/scan` for each selected repo
4. Shows scanning progress (reuse existing onboard progress UI)
5. Results grouped by source repo: "From `myapp` → express 4.18, pg 8.11, ..." with checkboxes
6. "Track Selected" button → calls existing `POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply`

**Fallback for unauthenticated users:**
- Show the existing trending discovery carousel (no change)

Does this flow make sense?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

1. **Stars tab** — grid of starred repos with Track buttons, already-tracked repos dimmed with checkmark
2. **Dependencies tab** — repo picker with checkboxes sorted by last push, "Scan Selected" button

**Component breakdown:**
- `SuggestionsSection` — replaces `DiscoverySection` for authenticated users. Contains tab state and delegates to child components.
- `StarsTab` — fetches `/api/v1/suggestions/stars`, renders repo cards with track buttons. Reuses the one-click track logic from the existing discovery carousel.
- `DepsTab` — multi-step: repo picker → scanning progress → results with track checkboxes. Reuses the existing onboard scan API and progress UI patterns.

**Backend:**
- New file `internal/api/suggestions.go` — two handlers (`handleSuggestStars`, `handleSuggestRepos`) that proxy GitHub public API with caching.
- Registered on the existing router, auth-gated.

**No new database tables. No migrations. No OAuth changes.**

## Design Section 4: Edge Cases & Error Handling

A few things to handle:

- **GitHub rate limiting**: Server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN` gives 5000 req/hr. Cache results per-user for 1 hour. If rate limited, show a friendly "GitHub API limit reached, try again later" message.
- **User with no stars**: Show an empty state — "You haven't starred any repos on GitHub yet" with a link to explore trending instead.
- **User with no public repos**: Same pattern — "No public repos found" with suggestion to use the quick onboard scanner instead.
- **Scanning failures**: Individual repo scans can fail (private repo mistakenly listed as public, empty repo, no dependency files). Show per-repo status — success/failed/no dependencies found. Don't block other scans.
- **Already tracked filtering**: Both tabs filter out repos/dependencies that are already tracked as sources. The stars tab dims them with a checkmark (shown in mockup). The dependencies results do the same.
- **Multiple concurrent scans**: The existing onboard scanner already prevents concurrent scans for the same repo (409 conflict). For multi-repo scanning, we queue them sequentially or in parallel depending on how many — cap at 5 concurrent scans to avoid flooding.

Anything you'd add or change here?

> DEVELOPER

looks good

{
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-23-personalized-suggestions-design.md",
  "content": "# Personalized GitHub Suggestions — Design Spec\n\n## Overview\n\nAdd personalized repo/dependency suggestions to the dashboard for GitHub-authenticated users. Replaces the generic trending discovery carousel with two tabs: \"Your Stars\" (user's starred repos) and \"Your Dependencies\" (scan user-selected repos for dependencies). Uses public GitHub API only — no OAuth changes needed.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **Data access**: Public GitHub API via server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN` (no per-user token storage, no OAuth scope changes)\n- **Placement**: Replaces trending carousel for authenticated users; unauthenticated users see existing trending carousel\n- **Trigger**: On demand — user clicks to fetch each tab's data\n- **Layout**: Two tabs — \"Your Stars\" | \"Your Dependencies\"\n- **Dependencies flow**: User picks repos from checklist → scan selected → review & track results\n- **Dependency scanning**: Reuses existing onboard scan pipeline (LLM extraction via `POST /api/v1/onboard/scan`)\n\n## Backend API\n\n### `GET /api/v1/suggestions/stars`\n\n- Auth required (session cookie)\n- Fetches `GET https://api.github.com/users/{github_login}/starred` using server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN`\n- Paginates up to ~100 results (GitHub returns 30/page)\n- Caches per-user for 1 hour (keyed by `github_login`)\n- Filters out repos already tracked as sources\n- Response: array of `{ name, full_name, description, stars, language, url, avatar_url, provider: \"github\" }`\n\n### `GET /api/v1/suggestions/repos`\n\n- Auth required (session cookie)\n- Fetches `GET https://api.github.com/users/{github_login}/repos?sort=pushed&per_page=100`\n- Caches per-user for 1 hour\n- Response: array of `{ name, full_name, description, language, url, pushed_at }`\n\n### Dependency scanning\n\nReuses existing endpoints — no new endpoints needed:\n- `POST /api/v1/onboard/scan` — start scan for a repo\n- `GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}` — poll scan status\n- `POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply` — apply selections (create projects + sources)\n\n## Frontend Components\n\n### SuggestionsSection\n\nTop-level component that replaces `DiscoverySection` for authenticated users. Manages tab state (\"stars\" | \"deps\"). Falls back to `DiscoverySection` for unauthenticated users.\n\n### StarsTab\n\n- Initial state: \"Load your starred repos\" button\n- After fetch: grid of repo cards with name, description, star count, language badge\n- Already-tracked repos dimmed with checkmark\n- One-click \"Track\" button per repo (creates project + source + auto-polls, same logic as discovery carousel)\n- \"Show more\" pagination\n\n### DepsTab\n\nMulti-step flow:\n\n1. **Repo picker**: \"Load your repos\" button → fetches repo list → checkboxes sorted by `pushed_at` desc → \"Scan Selected (N)\" button\n2. **Scanning**: Progress indicators per repo (reuses onboard progress UI patterns). Cap at 5 concurrent scans.\n3. **Results**: Dependencies grouped by source repo (e.g., \"From myapp → express, lodash, pg\"). Checkboxes to select which to track. Dropdown to assign to existing project or create new one.\n4. **Applied**: Success confirmation with counts (reuses onboard applied state)\n\n## Edge Cases\n\n- **GitHub rate limiting**: 5000 req/hr with server-side token. Cache 1hr per user. Show friendly error if rate limited.\n- **No stars / no repos**: Empty state messages with suggestion to try trending discovery or quick onboard instead.\n- **Scan failures**: Per-repo status (success/failed/no deps). Failed scans don't block others.\n- **Already tracked**: Both tabs filter/dim repos and dependencies already tracked as sources.\n- **Concurrent scans**: Cap at 5 concurrent per user. Existing 409 conflict handling for duplicate repo scans.\n\n## Files to Create/Modify\n\n### New files\n- `internal/api/suggestions.go` — two handlers + GitHub API client functions + caching\n- `web/components/dashboard/suggestions-section.tsx` — top-level tabbed component\n- `web/components/dashboard/stars-tab.tsx` — stars tab with repo cards\n- `web/components/dashboard/deps-tab.tsx` — dependencies tab with multi-step flow\n\n### Modified files\n- `internal/api/server.go` — register new `/api/v1/suggestions/` routes\n- `web/lib/api/client.ts` — add `suggestions.stars()` and `suggestions.repos()` client methods\n- `web/app/page.tsx` — conditionally render `SuggestionsSection` vs `DiscoverySection` based on auth state\n\n## Non-goals (future work)\n\n- Private repo access (requires storing per-user OAuth tokens, expanded scopes)\n- Auto-fetch on first login\n- Recommendation ranking / ML-based suggestions\n- Non-GitHub providers (GitLab, Bitbucket stars/repos)\n"
}
```

I now have a thorough understanding of the codebase and the spec. Let me compile the review.

---

## **Issues Found**

### Critical Issues

**1. NO_AUTH / dev mode makes the suggestions endpoints non-functional.**

In `NO_AUTH` mode, the `chain` middleware is `publicChain` (no auth), meaning `auth.UserFromContext(r.Context())` returns `nil` -- there is no user in context. The spec says the new endpoints use "Auth required (session cookie)" and will extract `github_login` from the session user, but it does not address how this works in `NO_AUTH` mode. The existing `Auth` middleware is entirely skipped when `deps.NoAuth` is true (see `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/server.go` line 42-45). The dev user has `GitHubLogin: "dev"`, and calling `https://api.github.com/users/dev/starred` would return some random GitHub user's stars, not the developer's.

**Recommendation**: The spec must explicitly state how the suggestions routes behave in `NO_AUTH` mode. Options: (a) register them on `chain` (auth-required) even in `NO_AUTH` mode -- but then they fail since `chain == publicChain`; (b) add special-case handling in the handler to check if user is nil or login is "dev" and return an empty result or an error; (c) use the `DevSession` middleware wrapper for these specific routes in `NO_AUTH` mode. This needs to be specified.

**2. The `/api/v1/suggestions/stars` endpoint relies on starred repos being public, but the GitHub API endpoint `GET /users/{username}/starred` only returns *public* stars -- this is correct for a server-side token, but this limitation is not documented.**

The spec says "Public GitHub API" and "no per-user token storage", which means the server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN` authenticates the request for rate-limit purposes, but since it is not the user's own token, only the user's publicly-visible stars are returned. If a user has starred private repos, those will not appear. This is probably fine but should be explicitly called out as a known limitation.

**3. The `/api/v1/suggestions/repos` endpoint uses `GET /users/{github_login}/repos` which only returns the user's *public* repositories.**

Same issue as above. The `GET /users/{username}/repos` endpoint only lists public repos when authenticated with a different user's token. The spec should document this explicitly. More importantly: for the dependency scanning use case, users likely want to scan their *private* repos too (that is where most real dependencies live). The spec should acknowledge this limitation clearly in the "Non-goals" section or "Edge Cases."

**4. The spec says "Filters out repos already tracked as sources" for the stars endpoint but does not specify the mechanism.**

The handler needs access to the `SourcesStore` to query which repositories are already tracked. The spec lists `internal/api/suggestions.go` as the new file with "two handlers + GitHub API client functions + caching" but does not mention that the handler needs a `SourcesStore` dependency. Looking at how the `DiscoverHandler` is instantiated in `server.go` (line 134: `discover := NewDiscoverHandler(deps.HTTPClient, "", "")`), the suggestions handler will need more dependencies.

**Recommendation**: The spec should state that `NewSuggestionsHandler` takes `deps.HTTPClient`, `deps.SourcesStore`, and optionally a `GITHUB_TOKEN` string.

### Moderate Issues

**5. Missing specification of how to extract `github_login` from the request context in the handler.**

The spec says "Auth required (session cookie)" but does not specify that the handler calls `auth.UserFromContext(r.Context())` to get the `*auth.User` and reads `user.GitHubLogin`. This is an implementation detail, but since the spec enumerates the exact GitHub API URLs with `{github_login}`, it should state where that value comes from in the handler. More critically, when the request comes via Bearer token (API key auth), there is **no user in context** at all -- the `Auth` middleware (line 36 in `auth.go`) calls `next.ServeHTTP(w, r)` without injecting a user. API key users cannot use these endpoints. The spec should note this.

**6. The response format for `/api/v1/suggestions/stars` does not wrap in the standard `ApiResponse` envelope.**

Looking at the existing API client (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/client.ts`), all responses use `ApiResponse<T>` which wraps data in `{ data: T }`. The spec's response description says "array of `{ name, full_name, ... }`" but doesn't clarify whether it follows the standard envelope pattern. The frontend client modifications listed (`suggestions.stars()` and `suggestions.repos()`) need to know the response shape.

**Recommendation**: Explicitly state the response uses `ApiResponse<[]DiscoverItem>` format consistent with other endpoints, or define a new response type.

**7. The spec says "Already-tracked repos dimmed with checkmark" in StarsTab but the filtering happens both server-side ("Filters out repos already tracked as sources") and client-side ("Already-tracked repos dimmed").**

These are contradictory. If the server filters them out, the client will never see them to dim. If the intent is to dim rather than hide, the server should return them with a `tracked: true` flag instead of filtering. The spec needs to pick one approach.

**8. Missing pagination details for the `/api/v1/suggestions/repos` endpoint.**

The stars endpoint says "Paginates up to ~100 results (GitHub returns 30/page)" which implies the server fetches multiple pages from GitHub and aggregates. The repos endpoint just says `per_page=100` in the URL. The spec should clarify: does the server do multi-page fetching for repos too? GitHub caps `per_page` at 100, so users with more than 100 repos will see a truncated list. Is that acceptable? Should there be cursor-based pagination on the Changelogue API side?

**9. Cache invalidation not addressed.**

The spec says "Caches per-user for 1 hour" for both endpoints. But if a user tracks a repo from the stars list, the cached results will still show that repo for up to an hour (even if server-side filtering is applied, the cache would need to be invalidated or the filtering must happen post-cache). The spec should clarify whether filtering happens before or after the cache, or whether tracking a repo invalidates the cache.

### Minor Issues

**10. The spec says the frontend falls back to `DiscoverySection` for "unauthenticated users" but the auth context in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/auth/context.tsx` redirects unauthenticated users to `/login` (line 65).** 

If unauthenticated users are always redirected to `/login`, they will never see the dashboard page at all. The "unauthenticated fallback" scenario only applies in `NO_AUTH` dev mode (where `user` is always set). The spec's framing about "unauthenticated users see existing trending carousel" is misleading given the actual auth flow.

**Recommendation**: Reframe as "Users without a GitHub login (e.g., API key auth or dev mode) see the existing trending carousel."

**11. The "Files to Create/Modify" section does not mention adding `GITHUB_TOKEN` to the `SuggestionsHandler` initialization.**

The discover handler currently does not use `GITHUB_TOKEN`. The ingestion layer and onboard scanner read it from `os.Getenv` directly. The spec should clarify whether the suggestions handler also reads from env directly (like the onboard scanner) or receives it as a constructor parameter (more testable).

**12. No error response format specified.**

The spec says "Show friendly error if rate limited" for GitHub rate limiting but does not specify the HTTP status code or error response body the Changelogue API should return. Looking at existing patterns in `discover.go`, the convention is `RespondError(w, r, http.StatusBadGateway, "upstream_error", ...)`. The spec should match this.

**13. The spec does not mention tests.**

No test file is listed in the "Files to Create/Modify" section. Given the existing `discover_test.go` likely exists and the pattern of thorough testing in the codebase, the spec should call out `internal/api/suggestions_test.go` as a new file.

---

**Verdict: Issues Found** -- 13 issues identified. Issues 1, 4, 5, and 7 are the most important to resolve before implementation, as they would cause confusion or bugs during development. The rest are specification gaps that a senior implementer could figure out from context but would benefit from explicit documentation.

  "content": "# Personalized GitHub Suggestions — Design Spec\n\n## Overview\n\nAdd personalized repo/dependency suggestions to the dashboard for GitHub-authenticated users. Replaces the generic trending discovery carousel with two tabs: \"Your Stars\" (user's starred repos) and \"Your Dependencies\" (scan user-selected repos for dependencies). Uses public GitHub API only — no OAuth changes needed.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **Data access**: Public GitHub API via server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN` (no per-user token storage, no OAuth scope changes)\n- **Placement**: Replaces trending carousel for authenticated users; unauthenticated users see existing trending carousel\n- **Trigger**: On demand — user clicks to fetch each tab's data\n- **Layout**: Two tabs — \"Your Stars\" | \"Your Dependencies\"\n- **Dependencies flow**: User picks repos from checklist → scan selected → review & track results\n- **Dependency scanning**: Reuses existing onboard scan pipeline (LLM extraction via `POST /api/v1/onboard/scan`)\n\n## Known Limitations\n\n- **Public data only**: `GET /users/{username}/starred` and `GET /users/{username}/repos` with a server-side token only return publicly visible stars and public repos. Private stars and private repos are not accessible. This is by design for the current scope — private repo support is a future goal requiring per-user token storage.\n- **Repo cap**: GitHub API caps `per_page` at 100. Users with 100+ repos see only the most recently pushed 100. Acceptable for now.\n\n## Backend API\n\n### Handler Construction\n\n`NewSuggestionsHandler(httpClient *http.Client, sourcesStore db.SourcesStore, githubToken string)` — reads `GITHUB_TOKEN` from env at initialization (same pattern as onboard scanner). Needs `SourcesStore` dependency to check which repos are already tracked.\n\n### `GET /api/v1/suggestions/stars`\n\n- Auth required: extracts user via `auth.UserFromContext(r.Context())`, reads `user.GitHubLogin`\n- In `NO_AUTH` mode or when `github_login` is empty/`\"dev\"`: returns empty array (not an error)\n- API key auth (Bearer token): returns 403 — these endpoints require a session with a GitHub identity\n- Fetches `GET https://api.github.com/users/{github_login}/starred` using server-side `GITHUB_TOKEN`\n- Paginates up to ~100 results (GitHub returns 30/page, fetch up to 4 pages)\n- Caches raw GitHub response per-user for 1 hour (keyed by `github_login`). Tracked-repo filtering applied post-cache so tracking a repo is reflected immediately without cache invalidation.\n- Returns already-tracked repos with a `tracked: true` flag (not filtered out) so the frontend can dim them with a checkmark\n- Response: `ApiResponse<[]SuggestionItem>` where `SuggestionItem` is `{ name, full_name, description, stars, language, url, avatar_url, provider: \"github\", tracked: bool }`\n- Rate limit error: returns `502 Bad Gateway` with `{ error: \"upstream_error\", message: \"GitHub API rate limit exceeded, try again later\" }`\n\n### `GET /api/v1/suggestions/repos`\n\n- Same auth requirements as `/stars`\n- Fetches `GET https://api.github.com/users/{github_login}/repos?sort=pushed&per_page=100`\n- Caches per-user for 1 hour\n- Response: `ApiResponse<[]RepoItem>` where `RepoItem` is `{ name, full_name, description, language, url, pushed_at }`\n\n### Dependency scanning\n\nReuses existing endpoints — no new endpoints needed:\n- `POST /api/v1/onboard/scan` — start scan for a repo\n- `GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}` — poll scan status\n- `POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply` — apply selections (create projects + sources)\n\n## Frontend Components\n\n### SuggestionsSection\n\nTop-level component that replaces `DiscoverySection` for users with a `github_login`. Manages tab state (\"stars\" | \"deps\"). Falls back to `DiscoverySection` when `github_login` is absent (dev mode, API key auth).\n\n### StarsTab\n\n- Initial state: \"Load your starred repos\" button\n- After fetch: grid of repo cards with name, description, star count, language badge\n- Already-tracked repos dimmed with checkmark (using `tracked` flag from API)\n- One-click \"Track\" button per repo (creates project + source + auto-polls, same logic as discovery carousel)\n- \"Show more\" pagination (client-side, data already fetched)\n\n### DepsTab\n\nMulti-step flow:\n\n1. **Repo picker**: \"Load your repos\" button → fetches repo list → checkboxes sorted by `pushed_at` desc → \"Scan Selected (N)\" button\n2. **Scanning**: Progress indicators per repo (reuses onboard progress UI patterns). Cap at 5 concurrent scans.\n3. **Results**: Dependencies grouped by source repo (e.g., \"From myapp → express, lodash, pg\"). Checkboxes to select which to track. Dropdown to assign to existing project or create new one.\n4. **Applied**: Success confirmation with counts (reuses onboard applied state)\n\n## Edge Cases\n\n- **GitHub rate limiting**: 5000 req/hr with server-side token. Cache 1hr per user. Return 502 with descriptive error if rate limited.\n- **No stars / no repos**: Empty state messages with suggestion to try trending discovery or quick onboard instead.\n- **Scan failures**: Per-repo status (success/failed/no deps). Failed scans don't block others.\n- **Already tracked**: Stars tab returns `tracked: true` flag; frontend dims with checkmark. Deps tab results similarly indicate already-tracked dependencies.\n- **Concurrent scans**: Cap at 5 concurrent per user. Existing 409 conflict handling for duplicate repo scans.\n- **NO_AUTH / dev mode**: Suggestions endpoints return empty arrays when `github_login` is empty or `\"dev\"`. Frontend shows trending carousel fallback.\n- **Cache vs. tracking**: GitHub API response is cached; tracked-repo filtering is applied post-cache so it reflects immediately.\n\n## Files to Create/Modify\n\n### New files\n- `internal/api/suggestions.go` — `SuggestionsHandler` with `handleStars`, `handleRepos` + GitHub API fetching + per-user caching\n- `internal/api/suggestions_test.go` — unit tests for handlers (mock GitHub API, mock SourcesStore)\n- `web/components/dashboard/suggestions-section.tsx` — top-level tabbed component\n- `web/components/dashboard/stars-tab.tsx` — stars tab with repo cards\n- `web/components/dashboard/deps-tab.tsx` — dependencies tab with multi-step flow\n\n### Modified files\n- `internal/api/server.go` — register new `/api/v1/suggestions/` routes on auth-required chain, construct `SuggestionsHandler` with `deps.SourcesStore` and `GITHUB_TOKEN`\n- `web/lib/api/client.ts` — add `suggestions.stars()` and `suggestions.repos()` client methods\n- `web/app/page.tsx` — conditionally render `SuggestionsSection` vs `DiscoverySection` based on `github_login` presence\n\n## Non-goals (future work)\n\n- Private repo/star access (requires storing per-user OAuth tokens, expanded scopes)\n- Auto-fetch on first login\n- Recommendation ranking / ML-based suggestions\n- Non-GitHub providers (GitLab, Bitbucket stars/repos)\n"
}

### **1. Backend Handler Pattern**
**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/discover.go`

#### Constructor Signature (Lines 40-55)
```go
// NewDiscoverHandler returns a new DiscoverHandler. Empty URL strings default
// to the real GitHub / Docker Hub API base URLs.
func NewDiscoverHandler(client *http.Client, githubURL, dockerHubURL string) *DiscoverHandler {
	if githubURL == "" {
		githubURL = "https://api.github.com"
	}
	if dockerHubURL == "" {
		dockerHubURL = "https://hub.docker.com"
	}
	return &DiscoverHandler{
		client:       client,
		githubURL:    githubURL,
		dockerHubURL: dockerHubURL,
		cache:        make(map[string]cachedResult),
	}
}
```

#### Handler Struct (Lines 32-38)
```go
type DiscoverHandler struct {
	client       *http.Client
	githubURL    string
	dockerHubURL string
	mu           sync.Mutex
	cache        map[string]cachedResult
}
```

#### Response Type (Lines 14-24)
```go
type DiscoverItem struct {
	Name        string `json:"name"`
	FullName    string `json:"full_name"`
	Description string `json:"description"`
	Stars       int    `json:"stars"`
	Language    string `json:"language,omitempty"`
	URL         string `json:"url"`
	AvatarURL   string `json:"avatar_url,omitempty"`
	Provider    string `json:"provider"`
}
```

#### Caching Pattern (Lines 57-75)
```go
func (h *DiscoverHandler) getCached(key string) ([]DiscoverItem, bool) {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	c, ok := h.cache[key]
	if !ok {
		return nil, false
	}
	if time.Since(c.fetchedAt) > discoverCacheTTL {
		delete(h.cache, key)
		return nil, false
	}
	return c.items, true
}

func (h *DiscoverHandler) setCache(key string, items []DiscoverItem) {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	h.cache[key] = cachedResult{items: items, fetchedAt: time.Now()}
}
```

**Cache TTL Constant (Line 12):**
```go
const discoverCacheTTL = 1 * time.Hour
```

#### Handler Method Signature (Lines 78-158)
Example from `GitHub()` method:
- Takes `w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request`
- Extracts query params with `r.URL.Query().Get("q")`
- Uses `RespondJSON(w, r, http.StatusOK, items)` for response
- Uses `RespondError(w, r, status, code, message)` for errors
- Returns `[]DiscoverItem` wrapped by the response envelope

#### Route Registration (Lines 36-147)
Key excerpt for Discovery routes (Lines 133-136):
```go
// Discovery (public — no auth, proxies external APIs)
discover := NewDiscoverHandler(deps.HTTPClient, "", "")
mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/discover/github", publicChain(http.HandlerFunc(discover.GitHub)))
mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/discover/dockerhub", publicChain(http.HandlerFunc(discover.DockerHub)))
```

#### Middleware Chain Pattern (Lines 38-45)
```go
// Public chain: no auth required.
publicChain := Chain(RequestID, Logger, Recovery)

// Authenticated chain: includes auth unless NoAuth is set.
chain := publicChain
if !deps.NoAuth {
	chain = Chain(RequestID, Logger, Recovery, RateLimit(10, 20), Auth(deps.KeyStore, deps.SessionValidator))
}
```

**Key:**
- Public handlers use `publicChain`
- Private handlers use `chain`
- `Chain()` function composes middlewares in reverse order (see middleware.go)

### **3. Middleware & Response Helpers**
**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/middleware.go`

#### Middleware Chain Composer (Lines 11-20)
```go
type Middleware func(http.Handler) http.Handler

func Chain(mws ...Middleware) Middleware {
	return func(final http.Handler) http.Handler {
		for i := len(mws) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
			final = mws[i](final)
		}
		return final
	}
}
```

#### Built-in Middleware
- **RequestID** (Lines 22-28): Adds UUID to request context
- **Logger** (Lines 30-43): Logs method, path, status, duration_ms
- **Recovery** (Lines 55-65): Catches panics, logs, returns 500
- **CORS** (Lines 67-79): Sets standard CORS headers

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/response.go`

#### Response Helpers (Lines 56-81)
```go
// Standard response with envelope
func RespondJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, status int, data any) {
	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	w.WriteHeader(status)
	json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(envelope{
		Data: data,
		Meta: meta{RequestID: getRequestID(r.Context())},
	})
}

// Paginated response
func RespondList(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, status int, data any, page, perPage, total int) {
	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	w.WriteHeader(status)
	json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(listEnvelope{
		Data: data,
		Meta: listMeta{RequestID: getRequestID(r.Context()), Page: page, PerPage: perPage, Total: total},
	})
}

// Error response
func RespondError(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, status int, code, message string) {
	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	w.WriteHeader(status)
	json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(errorEnvelope{
		Error: apiError{Code: code, Message: message},
		Meta:  meta{RequestID: getRequestID(r.Context())},
	})
}
```

#### Pagination Helper (Lines 83-97)
```go
func ParsePagination(r *http.Request) (page, perPage int) {
	page = 1
	perPage = 25
	if v := r.URL.Query().Get("page"); v != "" {
		if p, err := strconv.Atoi(v); err == nil && p > 0 {
			page = p
		}
	}
	if v := r.URL.Query().Get("per_page"); v != "" {
		if pp, err := strconv.Atoi(v); err == nil && pp > 0 && pp <= 100 {
			perPage = pp
		}
	}
	return
}
```

#### Response Envelope Structure
```go
type envelope struct {
	Data any  `json:"data"`
	Meta meta `json:"meta"`
}

type meta struct {
	RequestID string `json:"request_id"`
}

type listEnvelope struct {
	Data any      `json:"data"`
	Meta listMeta `json:"meta"`
}

type listMeta struct {
	RequestID string `json:"request_id"`
	Page      int    `json:"page"`
	PerPage   int    `json:"per_page"`
	Total     int    `json:"total"`
}
```

#### One-Click Track Handler (Lines 54-89)
```tsx
const handleTrack = useCallback(
  async (item: DiscoverItem) => {
    const key = item.full_name;
    setTrackingIds((prev) => new Set(prev).add(key));
    try {
      const projRes = await projects.create({
        name: item.full_name,
        description: item.description,
      });
      const project = projRes.data;

      const provider = item.provider === "github" ? "github" : "dockerhub";
      const sourceRes = await sources.create(project.id, {
        provider,
        repository: item.full_name,
        poll_interval_seconds: 86400,
        enabled: true,
      });

      await sources.poll(sourceRes.data.id);
      await mutate("projects-list");
      await mutate("projects-for-dashboard");
    } catch (err: unknown) {
      const msg =
        err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Failed to track project";
      alert(msg);
    } finally {
      setTrackingIds((prev) => {
        const next = new Set(prev);
        next.delete(key);
        return next;
      });
    }
  },
  [],
);
```

**Pattern:**
1. Create project with `projects.create()`
2. Create source with `sources.create(projectId, sourceInput)`
3. Trigger initial poll with `sources.poll(sourceId)`
4. Revalidate SWR caches with `mutate()`

### **6. API Client Pattern**
**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/client.ts`

#### Base Request Function (Lines 31-42)
```typescript
const BASE = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL || "/api/v1";

async function request<T>(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<T> {
  const res = await fetch(`${BASE}${path}`, {
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...init?.headers },
    ...init,
  });
  if (!res.ok) {
    const body = await res.json().catch(() => null);
    throw new Error(body?.error?.message ?? `Request failed: ${res.status}`);
  }
  if (res.status === 204) return null as T;
  return res.json();
}
```

#### Discovery API Methods (Lines 239-253)
```typescript
export const discover = {
  github: (params?: { q?: string; language?: string }) => {
    const search = new URLSearchParams();
    if (params?.q) search.set("q", params.q);
    if (params?.language) search.set("language", params.language);
    const qs = search.toString();
    return request<ApiResponse<DiscoverItem[]>>(`/discover/github${qs ? `?${qs}` : ""}`);
  },
  dockerhub: (params?: { q?: string }) => {
    const search = new URLSearchParams();
    if (params?.q) search.set("q", params.q);
    const qs = search.toString();
    return request<ApiResponse<DiscoverItem[]>>(`/discover/dockerhub${qs ? `?${qs}` : ""}`);
  },
};
```

### **8. GitHub API Token Usage**
**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/onboard/scanner.go`

#### Scanner Constructor (Lines 47-56)
```go
func NewScanner(client *http.Client, baseURL, token string) *Scanner {
	if token == "" {
		token = os.Getenv("GITHUB_TOKEN")
	}
	if baseURL == "" {
		baseURL = "https://api.github.com"
	}
	return &Scanner{client: client, baseURL: baseURL, token: token}
}
```

#### Token Usage in Requests (Lines 160-177)
```go
func (s *Scanner) doGet(ctx context.Context, url string, out any) error {
	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, "GET", url, nil)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if s.token != "" {
		req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+s.token)
	}
	req.Header.Set("Accept", "application/vnd.github+json")
	resp, err := s.client.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
		return fmt.Errorf("GitHub API returned %d for %s", resp.StatusCode, url)
	}
	return json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(out)
}
```

**Pattern:**
- Read `GITHUB_TOKEN` from environment if not provided
- Set `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header
- Set `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` header

func TestDiscoverCaching(t *testing.T) {
	// Mock that counts calls
	var callCount atomic.Int32
	mockGH := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		callCount.Add(1)
		// ... response ...
	}))
	defer mockGH.Close()

	h := NewDiscoverHandler(mockGH.Client(), mockGH.URL, "")

	// First request
	w1 := httptest.NewRecorder()
	r1 := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/discover/github?q=cached", nil)
	mux.ServeHTTP(w1, r1)

	// Second request with same params
	w2 := httptest.NewRecorder()
	r2 := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/discover/github?q=cached", nil)
	mux.ServeHTTP(w2, r2)

	if calls := callCount.Load(); calls != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("expected 1 call (second cached), got %d", calls)
	}
}
```

`json:\"description\"`\n\tStars       int    `json:\"stars\"`\n\tLanguage    string `json:\"language,omitempty\"`\n\tURL         string `json:\"url\"`\n\tAvatarURL   string
